Home › Guide › F1 Weekend in Marbella 2025: Boat Rental Guide for the Race By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers The Formula 1 Spanish Grand Prix returns in 2025, and Marbella is the natural base for fans who want race-day adrenaline mixed with Mediterranean luxury. With the circuit in Barcelona just a couple of hours away, the smart move is to book an F1 weekend boat rental in Marbella – watch the race live on board, then celebrate with a cruise along the Costa del Sol. Here's how to make it happen. Why Marbella for F1 Weekend? Marbella is the playground of the rich and famous, and F1 weekend amplifies that energy. Puerto Banús fills with supercars, celebrities, and party-goers, while the marina buzzes day and night. You can drive to the circuit for the race itself, but the real spectacle is back on the coast – and from a boat, you get the best seat in the house. Anchor off the Golden Mile, watch the race on a screen, then dive into the crystal-clear water. Our Fleet for F1 Weekend We operate 29 boats from Puerto Banús, all with licensed skippers and full amenities. For F1 weekend, these are the standouts: Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) – €749/2h, €2,299/8h Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax) – €749/2h, €2,299/8h Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) – €4,719 minimum 4h, jet ski included free Mangusta 80 Grey (24m, 12 pax) – same terms, different livery All other boats (Azimut 58, Ferretti 94, Pershing 46, etc.) are available on request via WhatsApp – we'll quote within minutes. How to Watch the Race from a Boat You won't see the tarmac from the sea, but you can stream the race live on a tablet or TV while anchored in a calm bay. Bring your own device or ask us about onboard screens. The skipper will find a spot near Puerto Banús or Cala del Faro where the water is flat and the sun is warm. As the lights go out, raise a glass of cava – the sound of the engines is replaced by the gentle lapping of waves. F1 Weekend Itinerary Ideas Make the most of your charter with a structured day: Morning: Depart Puerto Banús at 10:00, cruise to Cabopino for a swim stop. Afternoon: Anchor for the race broadcast, enjoy snacks and drinks. Late afternoon: Head to Estepona or Cala Cortés for a final dip. Evening: Return to port, freshen up, and hit the Marbella nightlife. An 8-hour charter covers all of this comfortably. For a shorter option, a 2-hour sunset cruise is perfect for post-race celebrations. Pricing and What's Included Transparent pricing – no surprises. Tier A boats (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39) are €749 for 2 hours, up to €2,299 for 8 hours. The Mangusta 80 is €4,719 for a minimum of 4 hours, and that includes a jet ski for free. Every charter includes: Licensed skipper and fuel Water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks Insurance and safety equipment Spanish IVA (21%) Other boats are priced on request – message us on WhatsApp for a tailored quote. Beyond the Race: Coastal Highlights If you have extra days, extend your charter to explore the coast. Head to Sotogrande for upscale marinas, or the quieter Cabopino for a relaxed beach day. The views of La Concha mountain from the sea are unforgettable. Our skippers know the best anchorages – just ask. Frequently Asked Questions How far is the F1 circuit from Marbella? The Circuit de Barcelona-Catalunya is about 90–120 km from Marbella, roughly a 1.5 to 2-hour drive depending on traffic. Many fans stay in Marbella for the glamour and nightlife, then drive up for the race. Alternatively, you can watch the race live on board your charter while enjoying the Mediterranean. Can I watch the F1 race from a boat in Marbella? You won't see the circuit from the sea – it's inland. But you can watch the race broadcast on a large screen or tablet while anchored off Puerto Banús, with the sound of waves and a cold drink in hand. After the race, cruise to a beach club or Cala del Faro for a swim. Which boats are best for an F1 weekend party? TITLE: Marbella Family Boat Day: Paddleboarding and Snorkeling Combo Guide
DESCRIPTION: Plan a Marbella family boat day with paddleboarding and snorkeling. Rent a boat from Puerto Banús, enjoy calm coves, and book your charter today.

Home › Guide › Marbella Family Boat Day: Paddleboarding and Snorkeling Combo Guide By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning a Marbella family boat day paddleboarding snorkeling trip is one of the best ways to enjoy the Costa del Sol with kids. With calm coves, warm water, and a range of family-friendly boats departing from Puerto Banús, you can create a day that mixes adventure with relaxation. This guide covers the best boats, the top spots for paddleboarding and snorkeling, and what to expect from your charter. Why choose a boat day for paddleboarding and snorkeling in Marbella? A private boat gives you access to secluded coves that are hard to reach from shore. You avoid crowded beaches, set your own pace, and have all equipment on board. The Mediterranean water temperature in Marbella reaches 24°C in July and August, making snorkeling comfortable for all ages. Paddleboarding from a boat means you can explore quiet bays like Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés without fighting for space. Best boats for a family day on the water For small families (up to 5 people), the Dubhe (8m) or Mariah SX21 (6.4m) are nimble and easy to manage. For larger groups, the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) and Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax) offer more deck space and a cabin. These Tier A boats start at €749 for 2 hours or €2,299 for a full 8-hour day – all including skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, and VAT. If you want a bigger experience, the Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) comes with a free jet ski from €4,719 for 4 hours. All depart from Puerto Banús, Marbella . Top coves for paddleboarding and snorkeling Cala del Faro – A small, sheltered cove west of Marbella with clear, shallow water. Ideal for beginners. Cala Cortés – A rocky cove with good underwater visibility. Snorkelers often see small fish and octopus. Cabopino Beach – A sandy beach near the dunes, with calm conditions and a nearby marina. Río Verde area – Just east of Puerto Banús, this stretch has gentle currents and a sandy bottom. Your skipper will know the best spots based on wind and tide conditions. All are within a 15–20 minute cruise from Puerto Banús. What to bring for a family boat day Pack swimwear, towels, sunscreen (reef-safe if possible), hats, and sunglasses. Bring a change of clothes for after swimming. For kids, consider rash vests for extra sun protection. Paddleboarding and snorkeling gear can be arranged on request – just ask when booking. The boat provides water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, and light snacks. You can also bring your own food and drinks. Sample itinerary for a 4-hour family charter Time Activity 10:00 Board at Puerto Banús, safety briefing, depart 10:30 Anchor at Cala del Faro for paddleboarding 11:30 Snorkeling session in Cala Cortés 12:30 Cruise along the coast, enjoy drinks and snacks 13:00 Return to Puerto Banús For an 8-hour day, add a lunch stop at a beachside chiringuito or a longer swim break at Cabopino. Safety tips for paddleboarding and snorkeling with kids Always wear a life jacket when paddleboarding – the skipper will provide them. Snorkel in pairs, and stay close to the boat. The Mediterranean is generally calm, but currents can pick up near headlands. Listen to your skipper’s advice on where and when to go in. Most family-friendly spots have a sandy or rocky bottom with no strong waves. How to book your family boat day Contact us via WhatsApp for availability and pricing. We recommend booking at least 24 hours in advance, especially in July and August. Tell us your group size, preferred boat, and any extras like paddleboarding gear. We’ll confirm your charter and send departure details. All charters depart from Puerto Banús, Marbella’s premier marina. Frequently asked questions What boats are best for a family day on the water in Marbella? For small families, the Dubhe (8m, 5 pax) or Mariah SX21 (6.4m, 5 pax) are ideal. For larger groups, the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) or Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax) offer more space. All depart from Puerto Banús. Is paddleboarding equipment included in the charter? Paddleboarding gear is not automatically included but can be arranged on request. Let us know when booking, and we'll add a board to your charter. Snorkeling masks and fins are also available. Which coves are best for paddleboarding and snorkeling near Marbella? Cala del Faro and Cala Cortés are calm, sheltered coves perfect for beginners. Cabopino Beach and the area near Río Verde also offer clear water and gentle conditions. All are within a short cruise from Puerto Banús. How long should I book a boat for a family day? A half-day charter (4 hours) is enough for a relaxed morning or afternoon. For a full day (8 hours), you can explore multiple coves, enjoy a beach lunch, and fit in paddleboarding and snorkeling. Tier A boats cost €2,299 for 8 hours. What is included in the charter price? Every charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish VAT (21%). Just bring your swimwear and sunscreen. Can we bring our own food and drinks on board? Yes, you are welcome to bring your own food and drinks. The boat has a cooler and storage space. The included drinks and snacks are also provided, so you can supplement as you like. Is snorkeling safe for children in Marbella? Yes, the Mediterranean is generally calm, especially in summer. The skipper will anchor in shallow, sheltered spots. Life jackets are available for all ages. Always supervise children, and let the skipper know your experience level. How do I book a family boat day in Marbella? Contact us via WhatsApp for availability and pricing. Most boats require at least 24 hours' notice. TITLE: Feria San Bernabé 2025: Boat Party Guide from Marbella Marina
DESCRIPTION: Plan your Feria San Bernabé 2025 boat party from Marbella Marina. Check availability, prices from €749/2h, and top tips for a seamless day on the water.

Home › Guide › Feria San Bernabé 2025: Boat Party Guide from Marbella Marina By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Feria San Bernabé 2025 (9–15 June) is Marbella's biggest street party – think casetas, flamenco dresses, and fairground lights. But the real insider move? Pair the fair with a boat party from Marbella Marina or Puerto Banús. You get the best of both: a few hours of sun, sea, and cava, then a short taxi ride to the fairground for the evening. Here's how to plan it. Why a boat party works during Feria San Bernabé The fair is intense – crowds, noise, and heat. A boat trip gives you a cool, calm escape in the middle of the day. You can anchor off the coast, swim in the Mediterranean, and enjoy a chilled glass of white wine before hitting the casetas. Plus, you get a different perspective of Marbella from the water, with La Concha mountain as your backdrop. All our boats depart from Puerto Banús, just 15 minutes from the fairground. That means you can spend the morning on the water, return to port for a late lunch, and still make it to the fair by early afternoon. Dates and logistics for Feria San Bernabé 2025 Feria San Bernabé runs from Monday 9 June to Sunday 15 June 2025. The fairground is located in the centre of Marbella, near the old town. The opening night features a spectacular fireworks display, usually around midnight. If you're planning a boat party, aim for a mid-afternoon slot (13:00–17:00) to avoid the worst of the heat and traffic. From Puerto Banús, it's a 15-minute taxi ride (approx. €15–20). Alternatively, if you're staying on the Golden Mile, you're even closer – just 10 minutes to the marina and 15 to the fair. Our boats for your Feria boat party We have a range of vessels to suit different group sizes and budgets. What's included in your charter Every booking includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish IVA (21%). You won't pay extra for anything – just bring your swimsuit and sunscreen. For the Mangusta 80, a jet ski is also included free of charge – perfect for a bit of adrenaline between swim stops. Best anchorages near Marbella for a fair-day escape From Puerto Banús, you can head east towards Marbella's coastline or west towards Estepona. Our favourite spots: Cala del Faro – a quiet cove near Cabopino, ideal for swimming and snorkelling. Cala Cortés – a rocky bay with crystal-clear water, about 15 minutes from Puerto Banús. Río Verde – just off the Golden Mile, you'll see the luxury villas from the water. Marbella Bay – anchor right in front of the old town for a unique view of the fair fireworks. Your skipper will recommend the best spot based on wind and sea conditions. How to book for Feria San Bernabé Fair week is one of the busiest of the year, so book early. You can check availability and book directly on our website, or message us on WhatsApp for a quick response. For Tier A boats (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39), you can book online instantly. For larger yachts, we'll confirm availability and price within minutes. If you're planning a full day, consider an 8-hour charter (€2,299 for Tier A) – you can cruise to Estepona or Sotogrande for lunch, then return for the fair in the evening. Tips for a seamless fair + boat day Book a morning slot (10:00–14:00) to avoid the afternoon heat and fair traffic. Wear comfortable shoes for the fair – you'll be on your feet for hours. Bring a light jacket for the evening – sea breezes can be cool. If you're staying in Marbella city centre, taxi to Puerto Banús early to avoid fair-related road closures. Consider a sunset trip (19:00–21:00) to catch the fair lights from the water. Frequently asked questions When is Feria San Bernabé 2025? Feria San Bernabé 2025 runs from 9 to 15 June. The main fairground is in Marbella city centre, with casetas (marquees), rides, and live music. The fair typically kicks off with a fireworks display on the evening of 9 June, so plan your boat party either before or after the main events. How far is Puerto Banús from the Feria San Bernabé fairground? Puerto Banús is about 15 minutes by car or taxi from the Feria San Bernabé fairground in Marbella city centre. The marina is well-connected, and taxis are plentiful during the fair. If you're staying near the Golden Mile, you're roughly halfway between the two, making a boat trip even easier. Which boats are best for a Feria San Bernabé boat party? For a group of up to 12, the Mangusta 80 (€4,719 for 4 hours) offers plenty of deck space and a free jet ski. For smaller groups, the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 (€749 for 2 hours) are great value. All depart from Puerto Banús and include skipper, drinks, and snacks. Can I book a boat for just a few hours during the fair? Yes, Tier A boats (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39) are available from 2 hours at €749. Longer charters up to 8 hours are €2,299. For the Mangusta 80, the minimum charter is 4 hours at €4,719. Other boats like the Ferretti 94 or Canados 86 are priced on request via WhatsApp. What's included in the boat rental price? Every charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish IVA (21%). You won't have any hidden extras – just show up and enjoy the day. Is it better to do a boat party before or after the fair? A morning or midday boat trip is ideal before the fair heats up in the afternoon. You can anchor at Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés for a swim, then head back to Puerto Banús for a late lunch and taxi to the fairground. Evening trips are also popular for sunset views, but plan for fair traffic. How do I book a boat for Feria San Bernabé? You can check availability directly on our website or message us on WhatsApp for the fastest response. Home › Guide › Marbella Feria San Bernabe 2031: Best Boat Rental & Party Tips By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Marbella's Feria San Bernabe 2031 is the Costa del Sol's biggest summer party, drawing thousands to the streets of Marbella for a week of music, dancing, and celebration. But if you really want to do it in style, skip the crowded casetas for a few hours and take to the sea. Renting a private boat from Puerto Banús is the ultimate way to elevate your Feria experience — think champagne on the flybridge, swimming in crystal-clear coves, and arriving back just in time for the night-time festivities. This guide covers everything you need to know about combining a Marbella Feria San Bernabe 2031 boat rental with the fair, including the best boats, pricing, and insider tips. Why Rent a Boat During Feria San Bernabe? The Feria is fantastic, but it can be intense — hot afternoons, packed fairgrounds, and queues for drinks. A boat charter offers a perfect escape. You get: Privacy: Your own floating VIP lounge for up to 12 guests. Swimming: Anchor at Cala del Faro or Cabopino for a dip in the Mediterranean. Sunset views: Watch the sun sink behind La Concha mountain from the water. Convenience: Puerto Banús is just 10 minutes by taxi from the fairground. With all-inclusive pricing that covers skipper, drinks, and snacks, it's often cheaper than a night out on land — and infinitely more memorable. Our Fleet: Best Boats for the Feria All our boats depart from Puerto Banús , the glamorous marina just a short ride from Marbella town. URL: https://boatrentalinmarbella.com/blog/marbella-boat-rental-fishing-license-rules-2035/
TITLE: Fishing License Rules for Marbella Boat Rental in 2035
DESCRIPTION: Fishing license rules for Marbella boat rental in 2035: who needs one, how to get it, and what&#x27;s included. Book your charter today.

Home › Guide › Fishing License Rules for Marbella Boat Rental in 2035 By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning a fishing trip from Marbella in 2035? The rules are straightforward, but you need to get them right. In this guide, we cover who needs a fishing license, how to get one, what's included on our charters, and the key regulations you'll face. We'll also point you to the best boats for fishing – all departing from Puerto Banús. Who needs a fishing license in Marbella? In 2035, Spanish law requires a recreational sea fishing license for anyone aged 16 or older. This applies whether you're fishing from the shore, a kayak, or a chartered boat. Children under 16 can fish without a license, but only when accompanied by an adult with a valid license. The license is issued by the Junta de Andalucía and is valid for all coastal waters in the region. How to get your fishing license – step by step The good news: it's easy and cheap. Apply online via the Junta de Andalucía's official portal. You'll need your passport or NIE, and a credit/debit card. The fee is around €10–€15, and you'll receive your digital license instantly by email. Since 2030, paper licenses are no longer accepted – you must show the digital version on your phone or a printed copy. If you're not comfortable applying online, our team can help you before you board. Just arrive 30 minutes early and we'll walk you through the process. But note: we cannot issue the license – it's a government service. Fishing from our Marbella boat rental – what's included Every boat charter with us includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, and light snacks. For fishing, we also provide rods, reels, tackle, and bait – at no extra cost. The skipper will take you to the best fishing spots around Marbella, from the rocky bottoms near Cala del Faro to the deeper waters off Cabopino. Our most popular fishing boats are the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) and the Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax), both departing from Puerto Banús. For a more exclusive experience, the Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) offers a flybridge with panoramic views – perfect for spotting tuna schools. Fishing regulations you must know in 2035 Beyond the license, there are strict rules on what you can catch and keep. The Junta de Andalucía sets minimum sizes and daily bag limits. For example: sea bass minimum 36cm, bream 23cm, and bonito 25cm. Daily bag limits vary – typically 5–10 fish per species per angler. Our skippers know the current limits and will advise you on the spot. Spearfishing is strictly prohibited on our charters. Also, you cannot sell any fish caught – it's for personal consumption only. And remember: catch-and-release is encouraged for undersized fish, and our skippers are happy to help you handle them safely. What about fishing from a private boat vs. a charter? If you rent a boat without a skipper (bareboat), you still need the same fishing license. The license is tied to the angler, not the boat. However, we always recommend a skippered charter – not only for safety but because our skippers know the local waters and fishing grounds intimately. They'll put you on the fish. Best seasons for fishing in Marbella Fishing is good year-round, but the species change with the seasons. In spring (March–May), sea bass and bream are active near the shore. Summer (June–August) brings bonito and small tuna. Autumn (September–November) is prime time for larger tuna and amberjack. Winter (December–February) is quieter but still productive for bream and hake. Water temperatures range from 15°C in winter to 24°C in summer – so plan your clothing accordingly. And always book your charter in advance, especially in July and August when boats sell out. Our top boats for fishing trips All our boats are fishing-friendly, but some are better suited than others. For small groups, the Bandido (11.5m, 8 pax) is a sporty option with a large cockpit. For families, the Lagoon 380 (11.6m, 10 pax) is a stable catamaran – great for kids. And for a luxury day out, the Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax) offers unparalleled comfort. If you're after a specific boat, browse our full fleet . Frequently asked questions Do I need a fishing license to fish from a boat rental in Marbella? Yes, if you are 16 or older. Spanish law requires a recreational fishing license for all sea fishing, including from a charter boat. The license is issued by the Junta de Andalucía and must be carried with you (digital copy accepted). Children under 16 do not need a license when accompanied by a licensed adult. How do I get a fishing license for Marbella boat rental? Apply online through the Junta de Andalucía's website. You'll need your NIE or passport, and a payment method. The license costs around €10–€15 and is valid for one year. Processing is usually instant – you'll receive a digital copy by email. Print it or save it on your phone. Can I fish from any of your boats without a license? No. All anglers aged 16 and over must have a valid fishing license. Our skippers will ask to see it before departure. If you don't have one, we can help you apply online before your trip – but it's your responsibility to have it. What fishing gear is included on your charters? We provide rods, reels, tackle, and bait on all our boats – at no extra cost. Just bring your fishing license and sunscreen. Our skippers know the best spots for bream, sea bass, and bonito – and will happily help you set up. Are there size and bag limits for fish caught in Marbella? Yes. The Junta de Andalucía sets minimum sizes and daily bag limits for each species. For example, sea bass must be at least 36cm, and you can keep up to 5 per day. Our skippers will inform you of current limits – and we encourage catch-and-release for undersized fish. Is spearfishing allowed on your boat rentals? No. Spearfishing is not permitted on any of our charters. It requires a separate license and is strictly regulated. Our boats are for rod-and-line fishing only – which is safer and more relaxing for everyone on board. What happens if I forget my fishing license? You won't be able to fish. Our skippers are required to check licenses before departure. If you forget it, you can still enjoy the cruise, but no fishing lines will be deployed. We recommend saving your digital license on your phone before you arrive. Do you offer fishing-specific boat rentals? Yes. All our boats are available for fishing – from the Astondoa 40 to the Mangusta 80 . For serious anglers, we recommend the Azimut 39 for its open cockpit. Just let us know you're fishing when booking. ★★★★★ 4.9 · verified reviews From €749 2h skippered charter Direct quotes from our own Puerto Banús fleet — same boats, same skippers, no third-party hand-off. 💬 Message on WhatsApp Avg reply < 5 min · No deposit until you confirm 📅 Book now Browse the full fleet → Compare live prices on BoatHire24 → All charters include Licensed skipper & fuel Drinks: water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks Insurance & safety equipment Snorkel gear & inflatables VAT 21% — no surprises

Understanding the boat rental group size limits Marbella 2043 is essential for a smooth, safe, and legal charter. Each vessel in our fleet has a strict maximum passenger capacity, set by Spanish maritime law and the boat's safety certification. Whether you're celebrating a birthday, hosting a corporate event, or simply enjoying the Mediterranean with friends, knowing these limits helps you choose the right boat. Below, we break down the capacities for all our yachts, the reasoning behind the rules, and how to book without surprises. Why Group Size Limits Exist Group size limits are not arbitrary. They are based on the boat's design, stability, lifesaving equipment (life rafts, life jackets), and engine power. Spanish regulations (Real Decreto 876/2014) mandate that every recreational vessel has a certified maximum number of persons on board. Exceeding this limit voids insurance, risks capsizing, and can lead to fines of up to €6,000. Our charters always include a licensed skipper who enforces these limits. Frequently Asked Questions What is the maximum group size for boat rental in Marbella in 2043? The maximum group size is 12 passengers, as per Spanish maritime regulations. Larger yachts like the Ferretti 94 (29m) or Mangusta 80 (24m) can carry 12 pax. Smaller boats like the Dubhe (8m) are limited to 5 pax. Can I exceed the group size limit if I pay extra? No. Group size limits are set by law and safety regulations, not by price. Overloading a boat risks penalties and invalidates insurance. All charters must adhere to the certified passenger capacity. How many passengers can the Azimut 39 carry? The Azimut 39 (12.5m) carries up to 11 passengers. Yes, children of any age count as passengers. The total number of people on board, including infants, must not exceed the boat's certified capacity. Life jackets for children are provided. No single boat in our fleet can carry 15 passengers. For larger groups, you would need to charter two boats simultaneously. Contact us via WhatsApp for custom multi-boat arrangements. Do the group size limits apply to the skipper? No. The skipper is not counted in the passenger capacity. The limits refer strictly to guests. Our licensed skipper is included with every charter and ensures safety compliance. What happens if my group exceeds the limit on the day? The charter will not depart until the group size matches the boat's certified capacity. Any extra guests will be asked to disembark. No refunds will be given for non-compliance. Practical Tips for Decorating a Boat in Puerto Banús Boats have unique constraints. Follow these rules to keep your decorations safe and the skipper happy: No tape on paint or varnish – use string, magnets, or suction cups for hanging items. No glass – bring plastic cups, plates, and bottles. The boat’s motion makes glass a hazard. Secure everything – wind and movement can send loose items overboard. Use weights or ties. Keep the walkways clear – the crew needs access to ropes and controls. Decorate the saloon and seating areas instead. Bring a bin bag – leave the boat as you found it. Our crew will help with cleanup, but it’s your responsibility. Which Boat Is Best for a Hen Party in Marbella? TITLE: Marbella Boat Rental License Requirements in 2025: What You Need
DESCRIPTION: Do you need a boat licence to rent a boat in Marbella in 2025? Most skippered charters don&#x27;t require one. See the rules, exceptions, and options.

Home › Guide › Marbella Boat Rental License Requirements in 2025: What You Need By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning a day on the water in Marbella? The first question most people ask is: do I need a boat licence to rent a boat in Marbella in 2025? The short answer: no , if you book a skippered charter. All our boats come with a licensed skipper, so you can simply step aboard and enjoy. But if you're considering a bareboat rental or want to know the rules for driving yourself, here's everything you need to know. Do you need a licence for a skippered boat rental? No. When you rent a boat with a skipper, the captain is responsible for navigation, safety, and docking. You don't need any licence, not even a basic one. This is the most common way to charter in Puerto Banús, and it's perfect for families, groups, and celebrations. You can still take the helm under the skipper's supervision if you want a go. All our charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, and safety equipment. The Spanish IVA (21%) is also included in the price. So the only thing you need to bring is sunscreen. What about bareboat rental (no skipper)? If you want to rent a boat without a skipper, you'll need a valid boating licence. In Spain, the requirements depend on the boat's length and engine power. For boats up to 6m and 40kW, a basic 'Licencia de Navegación' suffices. For boats up to 8m, you need a PER (Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo). For boats up to 15m, you need a PPER (Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo) or an acceptable equivalent. Note: We don't offer bareboat rentals. All our boats, from the 6.4m Mariah SX21 to the 29m Ferretti 94, are skippered. This ensures your safety and lets you relax. Spanish boating licences explained (2025) Spain has a tiered system for recreational boating licences. Here are the main ones: Licencia de Navegación – for boats up to 6m and 40kW (approx. 54hp). Minimum age 16. Requires a short theory test and practical training. Patrón de Navegación Básica (PNB) – for boats up to 8m and 40kW, and jet skis. Minimum age 16. More comprehensive than the Licencia. Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo (PER) – for boats up to 8m and 12 nautical miles from shore. Minimum age 18. This is the most popular licence for recreational sailors. Patrón de Yate – for boats up to 15m. Requires a PER as a prerequisite. Capitan de Yate – for boats up to 24m. Advanced qualification. For non-residents, the International Certificate of Competence (ICC) is generally accepted for boats up to 15m, provided it's issued by a recognised authority (e.g., the RYA in the UK). What licence do I need for a specific boat size? Boat length Required licence (Spain) International equivalent Up to 6m Licencia de Navegación ICC Up to 8m PER or PNB ICC Up to 15m PPER or Patrón de Yate ICC (up to 15m) Over 15m Capitan de Yate (or higher) Not typically accepted As you can see, most bareboat charters in Marbella are for boats under 15m. If you want a larger vessel, a skipper is mandatory. Do you need a licence for a jet ski in Marbella? Yes, to drive a jet ski independently you need a 'Licencia de Navegación' or a PNB. However, on our Mangusta 80 charter, a jet ski is included free of charge (minimum 4 hours, €4,719). The jet ski is used under the captain's supervision, so no extra licence is required for you. Every charter includes a licensed skipper, so you don't need any licence. Just bring your swimsuit and sunglasses. What about insurance and age requirements? All our charters include full insurance and safety equipment. The minimum age to charter a boat with us is 18, and children are welcome. There is no upper age limit. For skippered charters, there are no medical requirements beyond being fit to enjoy a day at sea. If you have specific questions about your situation, contact us via WhatsApp. We'll advise you on the best boat and any licence considerations (though for our charters, none are needed). Renting a boat in Marbella without a licence – step by step Choose your boat from our fleet – from the compact 6.4m Mariah SX21 to the 29m Ferretti 94. Pick a time slot – morning, sunset, or full day. We confirm availability and send you a quote. Show up at Puerto Banús, meet your skipper, and board. Enjoy the Costa del Sol – from Cala del Faro to Cala Cortés, or head to Cabopino or Estepona. No paperwork, no licence checks, no stress. Frequently asked questions Do I need a boat licence to rent a boat in Marbella? No, if you book a skippered charter. All our boats come with a licensed skipper, so you don't need any licence. You can relax and enjoy the coast. For bareboat (self-drive) rentals, you would need a valid licence, but we don't offer bareboat charters. What licence do I need to drive a boat in Spain? For recreational boating in Spain, you need a Spanish 'Licencia de Navegación' (up to 6m and 40kW), a 'Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo' (PER) for boats up to 8m, or a 'Patrón de Yate' for larger vessels. Non-residents can use an International Certificate of Competence (ICC) for boats up to 15m. Can I rent a boat in Marbella without a licence? Yes. The easiest way is to book a skippered charter: our captain handles everything. You can still steer under supervision if you like. This is the most popular option for tourists and requires zero paperwork. Is an ICC accepted for boat rental in Spain? Yes, the International Certificate of Competence (ICC) is accepted for bareboat charter in Spain for boats up to 15m (approx. 49ft). It must be issued by a recognised authority. For boats over 15m, you'd typically need a higher qualification. What is the PER licence in Spain? PER stands for Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo. It's the most common Spanish recreational boating licence, allowing you to operate boats up to 8m (26ft) within 12 nautical miles of the coast. It requires a theory exam, practical training, and a medical certificate. Can I rent a boat in Marbella with a foreign driving licence? No, a car driving licence is not valid for boats. You need a specific boating licence. However, if you book a skippered charter, no licence is needed at all. Do I need a licence for a jet ski in Marbella? Yes, to drive a jet ski independently you need a 'Licencia de Navegación' or a PNB (Patrón de Navegación Básica). But on our Mangusta 80 charter, a free jet ski is included and operated under the captain's supervision, so no extra licence is needed. Last updated: 2025. Our fleet includes 8 boats in this category, all departing from Puerto Banús, with prices that include fuel, drinks, snacks, and insurance. This guide breaks down the real costs, boat options, and what you get for your money – no fluff, just facts. Why You Don’t Need a Licence for Boats Under 12.5m Spanish regulations (Real Decreto 875/2014) state that recreational vessels under 12.5 metres in length can be operated by anyone without a specific boating licence, provided a licensed skipper is present. All our no-licence charters include a professional skipper who handles navigation, docking, and safety. You can relax, swim, or even take the wheel under supervision – but the skipper remains legally responsible. This setup is standard across Marbella and ensures full insurance cover.
